(2) Block opponent two-in-a-rows immediately during placement. (3) When removing an opponent piece, take pieces not in mills, prefer ones that threaten a mill. ## Minute 0, pass 3

**Input**


**Tool `writeBrain`**

```json
{
  "text": "Nine men's morris, side X. Core rules:\n1. Opening placement: prefer junction points with 4 neighbours (5, 11, 14, 20) and midpoints of outer lines (2, 10, 15, 23) before corners. Corners (1,3,22,24) have only 2 neighbours; avoid spending early pieces there unless forming a mill.\n2. Always check first: if opponent has two on a line with the third point free, block it (unless you can close your own mill this turn). Also check whether a placement creates a double threat (two open lines at once) — that wins material.\n3. When you close a mill, remove an opponent piece not in a mill, preferring one that is part of an open two-in-a-row or a piece that blocks your mill line or has high mobility.\n4. Keep pieces mobile: avoid positions where your pieces are boxed in, since no legal move = loss. Count free neighbours before each movement.\n5. Movement phase: try to shuttle one piece between two spots that each complete a mill (a \"running mill\"). Watch the opponent's shuttles and block the target point.\n6. With 3 pieces you can fly: keep fly threats in mind; with 4 vs 3 aim for double threats.\n7. Draw rules: 40 turns without a mill after placement and 3-fold repetition. If behind materially, vary moves to avoid repetition only if it does not lose; if ahead, avoid repetition-based draws.\n8. Each turn: viewBoard, propose move with reason, get vote, playMove exactly the number. After a mill, playMove the removal point."
}
```

-> Your team's brain is saved as version 1 (251 words).

> Your current brain:
> Nine men's morris, side X. Core rules:
> 1. Opening placement: prefer junction points with 4 neighbours (5, 11, 14, 20) and midpoints of outer lines (2, 10, 15, 23) before corners. Corners (1,3,22,24) have only 2 neighbours; avoid spending early pieces there unless forming a mill.
> 2. Always check first: if opponent has two on a line with the third point free, block it (unless you can close your own mill this turn). Also check whether a placement creates a double threat (two open lines at once) — that wins material.
> 3. When you close a mill, remove an opponent piece not in a mill, preferring one that is part of an open two-in-a-row or a piece that blocks your mill line or has high mobility.
> 4. Keep pieces mobile: avoid positions where your pieces are boxed in, since no legal move = loss. Count free neighbours before each movement.
> 5. Movement phase: try to shuttle one piece between two spots that each complete a mill (a "running mill"). Watch the opponent's shuttles and block the target point.
> 6. With 3 pieces you can fly: keep fly threats in mind; with 4 vs 3 aim for double threats.
> 7. Draw rules: 40 turns without a mill after placement and 3-fold repetition. If behind materially, vary moves to avoid repetition only if it does not lose; if ahead, avoid repetition-based draws.
> 8. Each turn: viewBoard, propose move with reason, get vote, playMove exactly the number. After a mill, playMove the removal point.

- Before each placement, list O pairs with empty third points and O fork points (a square that creates two pairs at once). Occupy fork points or make a mill threat that forces O to block elsewhere.
- Removing an O piece that blocks your line reopens your threat, forcing a reblock (tempo).

Movement:
- Best weapon: a mill that opens to a square O cannot reach. With 3-15-24 mine, moving 15->14 then 14->15 re-forms the mill; O cannot block 15 while 3, 14, 24 are yours. Repeat.
- Before opening a mill, check O has no one-move mill (two O pieces on a line, empty third, an O piece able to step in).
- If O opens its mill by moving a piece out, your re-closing mill should remove that out-moved piece so it cannot return.
- Pieces in a formed mill are protected; keep non-mill pieces as the ones O can take.
- O's typical shuttle is 19<->11 around 19-20-21; you often cannot block 19, so keep your own mill running.

Flying (3 pieces each):
- If both have 3, whoever mills first wins. Create a DOUBLE threat: e.g. 3,15 on 3-15-24 with 24 open; move the 24 piece to 1 so it threatens both 15->2 (1-2-3) and 1->24. O can block only one.

Draws: avoid repetition only when behind; watch the 40-turn no-mill counter.
Each turn: viewBoard, propose, vote, playMove. proposeMove on O's turn fails (NOT_ON_MOVE); wait.

> Nine men's morris, side X. Lessons from game 1 (won as X, turn 53).
> 
> Placement:
> - Good opening: 5, 14, then 2 and 3 to build lines.
> - Fork pattern worked: place 3 (threat on 1-2-3 forces a block at 1), then 15 makes a double threat on 13-14-15 and 3-15-24.
> - Before each placement, list O pairs with empty third points and O fork points (a square that creates two pairs at once). Occupy fork points or make a mill threat that forces O to block elsewhere.
> - Removing an O piece that blocks your line reopens your threat, forcing a reblock (tempo).
> 
> Movement:
> - Best weapon: a mill that opens to a square O cannot reach. With 3-15-24 mine, moving 15->14 then 14->15 re-forms the mill; O cannot block 15 while 3, 14, 24 are yours. Repeat.
> - Before opening a mill, check O has no one-move mill (two O pieces on a line, empty third, an O piece able to step in).
> - If O opens its mill by moving a piece out, your re-closing mill should remove that out-moved piece so it cannot return.
> - Pieces in a formed mill are protected; keep non-mill pieces as the ones O can take.
> - O's typical shuttle is 19<->11 around 19-20-21; you often cannot block 19, so keep your own mill running.
> 
> Flying (3 pieces each):
> - If both have 3, whoever mills first wins. Create a DOUBLE threat: e.g. 3,15 on 3-15-24 with 24 open; move the 24 piece to 1 so it threatens both 15->2 (1-2-3) and 1->24. O can block only one.
> 
> Draws: avoid repetition only when behind; watch the 40-turn no-mill counter.
> Each turn: viewBoard, propose, vote, playMove. proposeMove on O's turn fails (NOT_ON_MOVE); wait.

Game 2 is over and we lost: O won after 71 turns, reducing us to two pieces.

I've rewritten the team brain as version 3 (297 words, under the 300-word limit). The main changes:

- **Placement:** O can open at 5 and chain forcing threats, so after each forced block we should check whether our block also builds a pair of ours. Taking 15, 2 or 3 early is preferred over the reactive 14 opening.
- **Movement:** never let O keep a formed mill whose re-entry square we cannot reach. O's 1-2-3 mill shuttled through 15, and we had no piece next to 3 or 15, so O captured repeatedly. We should keep pieces on 14 or 24 and on 2 or 5.
- **Baiting:** I should not have played 14 to 6 as bait; O took the mill with 5 to 2 and removed my piece.
- **Blockade:** a blockade win is still possible, but each blocking move must not open a new exit for O.
- **Flying:** single threats get blocked, so a flying side needs a double threat in one move.

I posted the diagnosis to the team channel (two posts used) before writing the brain.
